\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{b^{2}\,\delta(b^{\ast})}{\lambda^{10}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

The rest of the terms in the parenthesis can be controlled in a similar fashion. Thus we have

|\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{2}|\lesssim\big\|{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{0}\big\|_{L^{2}}\bigg[\frac{b^{2}\,\delta(b^{\ast})}{\lambda^{10}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg)\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{b\,\delta(b^{\ast})}{\lambda^{8}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

(5.72)

For
\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{3}
, we compare it with the LHS of (
5.14
). The latter, according to the proof of Lemma
5.1
(see (
5.14
), (
5.17
)), has been splited into three separate parts in (
5.69
). Specifically,

\displaystyle\int{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{0}\cdot\Big({\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}{\rm R}_{z}\big[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\big]W+\big[\partial_{t},{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\big]W_{2}\Big)

(5.73)

\displaystyle=\int{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{0}\cdot\Big[-\mathbb{H_{\lambda}^{\perp}}\big(GW^{\perp}\big)+G{\rm R}W_{2}^{0}\,\Big]+\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{1}+\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{2}.

In view of the rough bound (
5.12
) and the above computations on
\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{1},\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{2}
, we have already estimated the integrals in (
5.73
) using Cauchy-Schwartz and Appendix
A
. Note that the first factor of their integrands are always
{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{0}
, bounded by

\int|{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{0}|^{2}=\frac{1}{\lambda^{6}}\int|{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}^{0}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{6}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

On the contrary, we review (
5.69
) and see the first factor of the integrand of
\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{3}
is
{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{1}
instead of
{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{0}
. This, by (
A.33
), actually gives a better bounded than
{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{0}
:

\int|{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{1}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{6}}\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}^{1}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{\delta(b^{\ast})}{\lambda^{6}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

The extra infinitesimal
\delta(b^{\ast})
bonus appearing here with the previous estimates yields the desired bound

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\Big|\hskip-1.00006pt\int\hskip-1.00006pt{\rm R}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}^{0}\hskip-1.00006pt\cdot\hskip-1.00006pt\Big[\hskip-1.00006pt-\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\mathbb{H_{\lambda}^{\perp}}\big(GW^{\perp}\big)\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptG{\rm R}W_{2}^{0}\Big]\Big|+|Q_{3}^{1}|+|Q_{3}^{2}|\bigg)

(5.74)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{b\,\delta(b^{\ast})}{\lambda^{8}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

Finally, for
\mathcal{Q}_{3}^{4}
, the smallness of
\|\hat{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}
(
5.44
) implies the same bound as (
5.74
) via similar analysis. Thus we have

|\mathcal{Q}_{3}|\lesssim\frac{b\,\delta(b^{\ast})}{\lambda^{8}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

(5.75)

Step 4
.
The term involving
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
. Recall the definition of
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
(
5.5
), we see the term involving
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
in (
5.23
) is actually given by

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\mathcal{Q}_{1}

(5.76)

\displaystyle=\int{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\Big(\partial_{t}\widehat{W}\wedge\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W+{\rm R}_{\hat{w}}\big[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\big]W\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{8}}\hskip-1.00006pt\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\cdot\hskip-1.00006pt{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\big(\partial_{s}\tilde{w}_{0}\hskip-1.00006pt\wedge\hskip-1.00006pt{\it\mathbb{H}}w\big)+\frac{1}{\lambda^{8}}\hskip-1.00006pt\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\cdot\hskip-1.00006pt{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\Big[\hskip-1.00006pt\big(\hskip-1.00006pt-\hskip-1.00006pt\rho_{1}w_{2}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\rho_{2}{\rm\hat{J}}w_{2}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptf\big)\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}w\Big]

\displaystyle+\int{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\Big(\widehat{W}\wedge\big[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\big]W\Big).

By the explicit formula of
w_{0}
given by Lemma
3.1
, and the modulation equations (
4.34
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\tilde{w}_{0}

\displaystyle=(\varLambda\chi)_{B_{1}}\bigg(\hskip-1.00006pt-\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{\partial_{s}B_{1}}{B_{1}}\bigg)w_{0}+\chi_{B_{1}}\partial_{s}w_{0}

(5.77)

\displaystyle=O\big(b\hskip 0.50003ptw_{0}\mathbf{1}_{y\sim B_{1}}\big)+\big(\partial_{s}w_{0}\mathbf{1}_{y\lesssim B_{1}}\big)=b^{2}\,O\Big(y^{3}\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}+y\log y\,\mathbf{1}_{1\leq y\lesssim B_{1}}\Big).

Applying this with (
5.77
), (
5.57
), (
A.6
), (
A.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\big(\partial_{s}\tilde{w}_{0}\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}w\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{2}\bigg(\int|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{4}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}w|^{2}|\log y|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+y^{4-2i})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

Also, by the double wedge formula, there holds

\displaystyle w_{2}\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}w

\displaystyle=\Big((e_{z}+\hat{w})\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}w\Big)\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}w

\displaystyle=(e_{z}\cdot{\it\mathbb{H}}w)\,{\it\mathbb{H}}w-|{\it\mathbb{H}}w|^{2}e_{z}+O\big(|\hat{w}|\hskip 0.50003pt|{\it\mathbb{H}}w|^{2}\big).

We apply (
A.3
), (
A.16
), (
A.18
), (
A.24
), (
A.25
) and it follows that

\Big|\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\big(w_{2}\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}w\big)\Big|\lesssim b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

The term involving
\rho_{2}{\rm\hat{J}}w_{2}
in (
5.76
) can be estimated similarly, and the one with
f
can be treated as in Lemma
5.2
. Now for last line of (
5.76
), we use (
5.71
) with (
A.3
), (
A.16
) to get

\Big|\int{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\Big(\widehat{W}\wedge\big[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\big]W\Big)\Big|\lesssim\frac{b\,\delta(b^{\ast})}{\lambda^{8}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

These yield that

\Big|\int{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\mathcal{Q}_{1}\Big|\lesssim\frac{b\,\delta(b^{\ast})}{\lambda^{8}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

(5.78)

Step 5
.
The integral involving
F
. From the definition (
4.27
), there holds the estimate:

\int{\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}W_{2}\cdot\big({\rm\hat{J}}_{\lambda}\mathbb{H_{\lambda}}\big)^{2}F\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{8}}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int\big|({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}f\big|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.79)

We claim the following bound:

\int|({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\widetilde{{\rm Mod}}|^{2}+\int|({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}|^{2}+\int|({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\mathcal{R}|^{2}\lesssim b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

(5.80)

In view of (
4.23
), we inject (
5.80
) into (
5.79
) and then the desired bound for the
F
integral follows. Collecting this with (
5.58
), (
5.68
), (
5.78
), (
5.75
), we conclude the proof of Proposition
5.1
. Now it remains to show (
5.80
).

Step 6
.
Contribution of
\widetilde{{\rm Mod}}
. We see from (
5.50
) that

\displaystyle{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\widetilde{{\rm Mod}}

\displaystyle=\chi_{B_{1}}\Bigg(\frac{a_{s}}{\rho_{1}^{2}+\rho_{2}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}-\rho_{2}\\
\rho_{1}\\
0\end{bmatrix}\varLambda\phi-\frac{b_{s}+b^{2}+a^{2}}{\rho_{1}^{2}+\rho_{2}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\rho_{1}\\
\rho_{2}\\
0\end{bmatrix}\varLambda\phi\Bigg)

\displaystyle+U(t)\hskip 0.50003pt\bigg(b\hskip 0.50003pty^{3}\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}+\frac{b\hskip 0.50003pty}{\left|\log b\right|}\mathbf{1}_{1\leq y\leq 6B_{0}}+\frac{\log y}{y}\mathbf{1}_{y\sim B_{1}}+\frac{1}{y^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{y\gtrsim B_{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+U(t)\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg[\hat{w}\wedge O\bigg(\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}+\frac{\log y}{y}\mathbf{1}_{1\leq y\leq 2B_{1}}+\frac{1}{y^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{y\gtrsim B_{1}}\bigg)\bigg].

Applying
{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}
again, we find in the region of
0\leq y\leq B_{1}
that the first line on the above RHS vanishes, and hence

\displaystyle\big({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\big)^{2}\widetilde{{\rm Mod}}

\displaystyle=U(t)\bigg(b\hskip 0.50003pty\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}+\frac{b}{y\left|\log b\right|}\mathbf{1}_{1\leq y\leq 6B_{0}}+\frac{\log y}{y^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{y\sim B_{1}}+\frac{1}{y^{5}}\mathbf{1}_{y\gtrsim B_{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+U(t)\hskip 1.00006pt{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\bigg[\hat{w}\wedge O\bigg(\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}+\frac{\log y}{y}\mathbf{1}_{1\leq y\leq 2B_{1}}+\frac{1}{y^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{y\gtrsim B_{1}}\bigg)\bigg].

For the first part above, a straightforward computation with (
5.49
) yields

U(t)^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\int\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\bigg(b\hskip 0.50003pty\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{b}{y\left|\log b\right|}\mathbf{1}_{1\leq y\leq 6B_{0}}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{\log y}{y^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{y\sim B_{1}}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{1}{y^{5}}\mathbf{1}_{y\gtrsim B_{1}}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\bigg)^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\lesssim b^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{4}}{\log M}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg),

while the estimate for the second part requires a separation by
\hat{w}=\tilde{w}_{0}+w^{\perp}+\gamma\hskip 0.50003pte_{z}
and further applications of the interpolation bounds in Appendix
A
. The resulting bound is identical. The details are left to readers. This yields the estimate for
\widetilde{{\rm Mod}}
.

Step 7
.
Contribution of
\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}
. According to (
5.4
), we let

{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}=\mathcal{P}_{1}+\mathcal{P}_{2},\quad\mbox{where}\quad\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{P}_{1}={\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hskip 0.50003pt\tilde{\varPsi}_{0},\\
&\mathcal{P}_{2}={\rm R}_{\hat{w}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hskip 0.50003pt\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

By (
2.19
), the explicit expressions are

\mathcal{P}_{1}=\begin{bmatrix}-\hskip-1.00006ptH\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(2)$}}}}}}\\
H\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\\
0\\
\end{bmatrix}-2(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\hskip 0.50003pt\partial_{y}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}\hskip 0.50003pte_{y},

(5.81)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\hat{\beta}\bigg[-\Delta\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}+2(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\Big(\partial_{y}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{Z}{y}\Big)\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\bigg]-\hat{\gamma}H\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(2)$}}}}}}\bigg\}\hskip 0.50003pte_{x}

(5.82)

\displaystyle+\bigg\{\hat{\gamma}\Big[H\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}-2(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\partial_{y}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}\Big]-\hat{\alpha}\bigg[-\Delta\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}+2(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\Big(\partial_{y}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{Z}{y}\Big)\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\bigg]\bigg\}\hskip 0.50003pte_{y}

\displaystyle+\bigg\{\hat{\alpha}H\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(2)$}}}}}}-\hat{\beta}\Big[H\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}-2(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\partial_{y}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}\Big]\bigg\}\hskip 0.50003pte_{z}.

Using (
3.56
), (
3.58
), (
3.60
), we have

\displaystyle\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{1}|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int\big|H^{2}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\big|^{2}+\int\big|H^{2}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(2)$}}}}}}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int\Big|\frac{\big(\partial_{y}+\tfrac{1}{y}\big)H\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(2)$}}}}}}}{1+y^{2}}\Big|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}|^{2}}{(1+y^{2})y^{6-2i}}\lesssim\frac{b^{6}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}},

which together with (
5.44
) yields

\int|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{1}|^{2}\lesssim\big(1+\|\hat{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{1}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{b^{6}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}.

Similarly, (
5.44
) implies

\int|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{2}|^{2}\lesssim\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{2}|^{2}.

(5.83)

Thus it suffices to control
{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{2}
directly. For this, we compute its components by (
5.82
), and estimate them respectively. For instance, let us consider for
y\leq 1
the following term coming from the first component of
{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{2}
:

\displaystyle H\bigg\{\hat{\beta}\bigg[-\Delta\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}+2(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\Big(\partial_{y}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{Z}{y}\Big)\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\bigg]\bigg\}

(5.84)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big|H\big(\hat{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt\Delta\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}\big)\big|+\Big|H\bigg[\hat{\beta}\big(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptO(y^{2})\big)\Big(\partial_{y}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{Z}{y}\Big)\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\bigg]\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{y|H\hat{\beta}|}{1+\left|\log y\right|}\Bigg(\sum_{1\leq i\leq 2}\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}|}{y^{4-i}}+\sum_{0\leq i\leq 1}\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}|}{y^{3-i}}\Bigg)+y\bigg(|\partial_{y}\hat{\beta}|+\frac{|\hat{\beta}|}{y}\bigg)\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}|}{y^{3-i}}.

Using (
3.55
), (
3.56
), (
A.11
), (
A.13
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{y\leq 1}\Big|H\bigg\{\hat{\beta}\bigg[-\Delta\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}+2(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\Big(\partial_{y}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{Z}{y}\Big)\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\bigg]\bigg\}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\frac{H\hat{\beta}}{y(1+\left|\log y\right|)}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}\Bigg(\sum_{1\leq i\leq 2}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(3)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(3)$}}}}}}|^{2}}{y^{8-2i}}+\sum_{0\leq i\leq 1}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}|^{2}}{y^{6-2i}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\|\partial_{y}\hat{\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}+\Big\|\frac{\hat{\beta}}{y}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}\bigg)\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}|^{2}}{y^{6-2i}}\lesssim\frac{b^{6}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}.

The estimates of (
5.84
) for
y\geq 1
can be derived in a similar fashion. Other terms in
{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{2}
can be also treated in the same way. Therefore we obtain

\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{P}_{2}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{b^{6}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}},

which together with (
5.83
) yields the bound for
\mathcal{P}_{2}
. This concludes the estimate for
\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}
.

Step 8
.
Contribution of
\mathcal{R}
. From the decomposition (
5.51
), and (
5.44
), we see

|({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\mathcal{R}|\lesssim|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1}|+|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}_{\hat{w}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1}|+|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{2}|+|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}_{\hat{w}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{2}|.

(5.85)

Using the estimate for
{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
5.52
), we compute by brute force that

\displaystyle{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1}

\displaystyle=O\big(H({\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1})\big)+O\bigg(\frac{\partial_{y}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1}}{1+y^{2}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1}}{y(1+y^{2})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg[\sum_{0\leq i\leq 2}\bigg(\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}Hw^{\perp}|}{y^{2-i}}+\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\Delta\gamma|}{y^{2-i}}\bigg)+\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}w|}{y^{3-i}(1+y^{2})}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times\bigg(b^{2}\hskip 0.50003pty^{3}\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{b^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\left|\log b\right|}\mathbf{1}_{y\sim B_{1}}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{b}{y}\mathbf{1}_{y\gtrsim B_{1}}\bigg),

where
b^{2}\hskip 0.50003pty^{3}\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}
eliminates the possible singularity at the origin caused by
|\partial_{y}^{i}w|/y^{3-i}
. From (
A.3
), (
A.5
), (
A.16
), all these terms can be controlled, and thus

\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1}|^{2}\lesssim b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

The estimate for
{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}_{\hat{w}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{R}_{1}
is more involved, but in view of the smallness of derivatives of
\hat{w}
indicated by (
A.15
), (
A.21
), (
A.22
), (
A.24
), it could be handled similarly. Therefore we have

\int|({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\mathcal{R}_{1}|^{2}\lesssim b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

Then for the terms involving
\mathcal{R}_{2}
in (
5.85
), we recall from (
5.51
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\rho_{2}{\rm\hat{J}}\big(w\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\big)-\rho_{2}w\wedge\big(\tilde{w}_{0}\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\big)

\displaystyle-\rho_{2}w\wedge\big(w\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\big)+\varTheta_{s}Z{\rm R}w.

The first two terms in
\mathcal{R}_{2}
are concerned with
{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}
, and thus easy to treat. Indeed, by (
5.53
),
{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}
would eventually become an extra
b^{2}
bonus before the
\mathcal{E}_{4}
bound, which results in the bound
b^{5}\delta(b^{\ast})/\left|\log b\right|^{2}
. Now we treat the third term in
\mathcal{R}_{2}
. By brute computations, and (
A.3
), (
A.11
), (
A.13
), (
A.16
), (
A.22
), we have

\displaystyle\int\big|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}

\displaystyle\it{\displaystyle\mathbb{H}}\big(w\hskip-1.00006pt\wedge(w\hskip-1.00006pt\wedge\hskip-1.00006pt{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0})\big)\big|^{2}\lesssim\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 4}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i_{1}}w|^{2}|\partial_{y}^{i_{2}}w|^{2}|\partial_{y}^{i_{3}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}|^{2}}{y^{2(4-i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3})}}

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{2}\hskip-3.00003pt\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 4}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i_{1}}w|^{2}|\partial_{y}^{i_{2}}w|^{2}}{y^{2(3-i_{1}-i_{2})}}\bigg(\mathbf{1}_{y\leq 1}+\frac{\log y}{y^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{1\leq y\leq 2B_{1}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{4}\bigg(\big\|\partial_{y}w\big\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\Big\|\frac{w}{y}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\bigg)\sum_{0\leq i\leq 4}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}w|^{2}}{y^{2(2-i)}(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}

\displaystyle+b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{4}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{2}w|^{4}}{(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{3}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg)\lesssim b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

Finally for the phase term
{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}(Z{\rm R}w)
in
\mathcal{R}_{2}
, to deal with the possible singularity at the origin, we split it into

\displaystyle{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}(Z{\rm R}w)

\displaystyle={\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}\big((Z-1){\rm R}w\big)+{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}({\rm R}w)

(5.86)

\displaystyle=-{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\big((Z-1)w\big)-{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{H^{\perp}}w.

For the first term in (
5.86
), we see

\displaystyle{\it\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\big((Z-1)w\big)

\displaystyle=H^{2}\big((Z-1)\alpha\big)e_{x}+H^{2}\big((Z-1)\beta\big)e_{y}

\displaystyle+2(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\Big(\partial_{y}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{Z}{y}\Big)H\big((Z-1)\alpha\big)e_{z},

where the singularity can only arise from the first two components, so we compute using the asymptotics
Z-1=-2y^{2}+O(y^{4})
for
y\leq 1
that

\displaystyle H^{2}\big((Z-1)\alpha\big)

\displaystyle\sim H^{2}(y^{2}\alpha)+O\big(H^{2}(y^{4}\alpha)\big)

\displaystyle=y^{2}H^{2}\alpha-4y\big(\partial_{y}H\alpha+H\partial_{y}\alpha\big)

\displaystyle-8H\alpha+8\partial_{y}^{2}\alpha+\frac{4\partial_{y}\alpha}{y}+O\big(H^{2}(y^{4}\alpha)\big).

The singular terms are actually

\displaystyle-4y\big(\partial_{y}H\alpha+H\partial_{y}\alpha\big)+\frac{4\partial_{y}\alpha}{y}

\displaystyle=-4y\bigg[2H\partial_{y}\alpha+\partial_{y}\bigg(\frac{V}{y^{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=8y\bigg(\partial_{y}^{3}\alpha+\frac{\partial_{y}^{2}\alpha}{y}\bigg)-4y\bigg[\,\frac{2V}{y^{2}}\partial_{y}\alpha+\partial_{y}\bigg(\frac{V}{y^{2}}\bigg)\alpha\,\bigg],

where by
V-1=4y^{2}+O(y^{4})
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2V}{y^{2}}\partial_{y}\alpha+\partial_{y}\bigg(\frac{V}{y^{2}}\bigg)\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{2(V-1)}{y^{2}}\partial_{y}\alpha-\partial_{y}\bigg(\frac{V-1}{y^{2}}\bigg)\alpha+\frac{2(Z-1)}{y^{2}}\alpha-\frac{2A\alpha}{y^{2}}

\displaystyle=O\big(\partial_{y}\alpha\big)+O(\alpha)-\frac{2A\alpha}{y^{2}}.

From (
A.10
), the last term here admits estimate

\int_{y\leq 1}\frac{|A\alpha|^{2}}{y^{4}}\lesssim C(M)\mathcal{E}_{4},

(5.87)

which helps control the singularity. For second term in (
5.86
), we can treat it in the same way, and the singularity may come from the third component, that is

\displaystyle\bigg(\partial_{y}+\frac{Z}{y}\bigg)H\alpha

\displaystyle=-\partial_{y}^{3}\alpha-\frac{1+Z}{y}\partial_{y}^{2}\alpha+\frac{V-1}{y^{2}}\partial_{y}\alpha-\frac{Z-1}{y^{2}}\partial_{y}\alpha+\partial_{y}\bigg(\frac{V-1}{y^{2}}\bigg)\alpha

\displaystyle+\frac{(Z-1)(V-1)}{y^{3}}\alpha+\frac{2(Z-1)}{y^{3}}\alpha+\frac{V-1}{y^{3}}\alpha+\frac{\partial_{y}\alpha}{y^{2}}-\frac{Z}{y^{3}}\alpha.

The only singular terms are the last two terms, which still constitute
-A\alpha/y^{2}
, and thus are bounded by (
5.87
) again. Other non-singular terms in (
5.86
) can be controlled by
C(M)\mathcal{E}_{4}
using (
A.3
). Then by the modulation equations (
4.34
), we have

\int\big|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}\big(\varTheta_{s}Z{\rm R}w\big)\big|^{2}\lesssim\big(|a|+U(t)\big)^{2}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg)\lesssim b^{2}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

The last term in (
5.85
) share the same bound as above due the smallness of
\hat{w}
. We omit the details. Therefore we obtain

\int|({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\mathcal{R}|^{2}\lesssim b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg),

(5.88)

which concludes the estimate for
\mathcal{R}
, and ends the proof.

\square

5.4
Closing the bootstrap bounds

In this subsection, we close the bootstrap argument by proving Proposition
4.1
. In particular, we apply Proposition
5.1
to show the refined bounds (
4.18
)–(
4.21
).

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Step 1
.
The refined bound for
b
. The modulation equations (
4.34
) implies the asymptotic

b(s)=\frac{1}{s}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{s\log s}\bigg).

(5.89)

This implies for
s_{0}\gg 1
chosen sufficiently large,
b(s)
decays over the rescaled time. In view of the self-similar transformation (
2.9
), we see for
t\geq 0
that

0<b(t)\leq b(0)<b^{\ast}.

Thus the refined bound (
4.18
) for
b(t)
holds for
K>2
.

Step 2
.
The refined bound for
a
. We define the function

\kappa(s)=\frac{2a(s)\left|\log b(s)\right|}{b(s)}.

In view of the assumption (
4.9
), we see
\kappa(s_{0})\in\mathcal{I}=[-\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2}]
(
t=0
when
s=s_{0}
), and the refined bound (
4.18
) for
a
is equivalent to
\kappa(s)\in\mathcal{I}^{\ast}=[-1,1]
for all
s\in[s_{0},+\infty)
. To show the latter, we compute the equation for
\kappa
using (
4.34
):

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\kappa(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{2a_{s}\left|\log b\right|}{b}-\frac{2ab_{s}\left|\log b\right|}{b^{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)

(5.90)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\left|\log b\right|}{b}\bigg[\frac{2ab}{\left|\log b\right|}+O\bigg(\frac{b^{2}}{\sqrt{\log M}\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{2a\left|\log b\right|}{b^{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)\bigg[b^{2}\bigg(1+\frac{2}{\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{b^{2}}{\sqrt{\log M}\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\kappa\hskip 0.50003ptb\big(1+o(1)\big)+O\bigg(\frac{b}{\sqrt{\log M}}\bigg).

From the largeness of
M
, we see
\kappa
is monotone increasing/decreasing near
\kappa=1,-1
, implying that if
\kappa
approaches the boundary of
\mathcal{I}^{\ast}
, it will escape from
\mathcal{I}^{\ast}
. We denote the moment when
\kappa
first leave by
s^{\ast}>0
, called the exit time. And the desired bound (
4.18
) holds if we prove
s^{\ast}=+\infty
. Let us consider the following subsets of
\mathcal{I}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}=\big\{\kappa(s_{0})\in\mathcal{I}:\hskip 0.50003pts^{\ast}\in(s_{0},+\infty)\mbox{ such that }\kappa(s^{\ast})=1\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{-}=\big\{\kappa(s_{0})\in\mathcal{I}:\hskip 0.50003pts^{\ast}\in(s_{0},+\infty)\mbox{ such that }\kappa(s^{\ast})=-1\big\}.

Now the problem can be categorized as three cases:
\mathcal{I}_{+}
empty,
\mathcal{I}_{-}
empty, and both of them being nonempty. In the first case, for any
\kappa(s_{0})\in\mathcal{I}
, we have
\kappa(s)<1
for all
s\in[s_{0},+\infty)
. We choose

\kappa(s_{0})>\frac{C}{\sqrt{\log M}},

(5.91)

for
C>0
sufficiently large. Then by (
5.90
),
\kappa
is monotone increasing, so that
\kappa(s)\geq\kappa(s_{0})>-1
, and thus
s^{\ast}=+\infty
. An analogous statement holds for the second case. In the last case, by the
\mathcal{C}^{1}
dependence of the solutions on the initial data, we know
\mathcal{I}_{+},\mathcal{I}_{-}
are open subsets of
\mathcal{I}
, and obviously disjoint. This implies, from an elementary topological argument on the connectedness, that
\mathcal{I}\neq\mathcal{I}_{+}\cup\mathcal{I}_{-}
. Consequently,

\exists\,\kappa(s_{0})\in\mathcal{I}\setminus(\mathcal{I}_{+}\cup\mathcal{I}_{-})\neq\emptyset,

(5.92)

satisfying
s^{\ast}=+\infty
, which is what we need. In summary, we can always find suitable initial data
a(t=0)
satisfying (
4.9
) such that the refined bound (
4.18
) for
a(t)
holds, concluding the proof of (
4.18
).

Step 3
.
The refined bound for
\mathcal{E}_{1}
. For any given vector under the Frenet basis

z=\hat{\alpha}\hskip 0.50003pte_{r}+\hat{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pte_{\tau}+(1+\hat{\gamma})\hskip 0.50003ptQ,

satisfying the constraint (
2.8
), the corresponding Dirichlet energy is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(z)

\displaystyle=\int|\nabla z|^{2}=-\int z\cdot\big(\Delta z+|\nabla Q|^{2}z\big)+\int|\nabla Q|^{2}|z|^{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\hat{\alpha},H\hat{\alpha}\big)+\big(\hat{\beta},H\hat{\beta}\big)+\big(-\hskip-1.00006pt\Delta\hat{\gamma},\hat{\gamma}\big)

\displaystyle+2\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\bigg(-\hat{\alpha}\hskip 0.50003pt\partial_{y}\hat{\gamma}+\hat{\gamma}\Big(\partial_{y}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{Z}{y}\Big)\hat{\alpha}\bigg)+\mathcal{E}(Q),

where we haved used (
2.16
). By Lemma
3.2
, straightforward computations show for any
s\in[s_{0},+\infty)
that

\big|\mathcal{E}(Q+\tilde{w}_{0})-\mathcal{E}(Q)\big|(s)\lesssim\sqrt{b}.

Then, in view of (
2.19
), the Dirichlet energy of the solution
u
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(u)(s)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}(Q+\tilde{w}_{0})+\big(w,{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\big)+\big(\tilde{w}_{0},{\it\mathbb{H}}w\big)+\big(w,{\it\mathbb{H}}w\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}(Q)+\big(\alpha,H\alpha\big)+\big(\beta,H\beta\big)+O(\sqrt{b}),

where the cross terms are controlled by
O(\sqrt{b})
via the bootstrap bounds. Also, the dissipative property (
1.3
) of the Dirichlet energy implies

\big(\alpha,H\alpha\big)+\big(\beta,H\beta\big)\leq\big(\alpha,H\alpha\big)(s_{0})+\big(\beta,H\beta\big)(s_{0})+O(\sqrt{b}).

(5.93)

Moreover, owing to the orthogonality (
4.4
), we have the coercivity estimate

\big(\alpha,H\alpha\big)+\big(\beta,H\beta\big)\geq C(M)\mathcal{E}_{1}(s),

(5.94)

for some universal constant
C(M)>0
independent of
K
, and for any
s\in[s_{0},+\infty)
. Its opposite direction follows directly from integration by parts

\big(\alpha,H\alpha\big)+\big(\beta,H\beta\big)\leq\mathcal{E}_{1}(s).

(5.95)

Now putting together (
5.93
), (
5.94
), (
5.95
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(s)\lesssim\big(\alpha,H\alpha\big)+\big(\beta,H\beta\big)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1}(0)+O(\sqrt{b})\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast}).

Note that the implicit constant in this inequality does not depend on
K
. Therefore by choosing
K
large enough, the refined
\mathcal{E}_{1}
bound (
4.19
) is proved.

Step 4
.
The refined bound of
\mathcal{E}_{4}
. A direct integration of (
5.55
) with respect to
t
shows
there exists constant
d_{2}\in(0,1)
and some constant
C>0
independent of
M
such that

\mathcal{E}_{4}(t)\leq\frac{\lambda(t)^{6}}{\lambda(0)^{6}}\mathcal{E}_{4}(0)+\big(2(1-d_{2})K+C\big)\lambda(t)^{6}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{b^{5}}{\lambda^{8}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}d\sigma.

(5.96)

To treat the first part on the RHS, we let
C_{1},C_{2}>0
be two universal constants large enough compared with the implicit constant in (
4.34
), and define

\eta_{1}=2-\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{\log M}},\quad\eta_{2}=2+\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{\log M}}.

Using the modulation equations (
4.34
), we compute the following derivative:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{b\left|\log b\right|^{\eta_{i}}}{\lambda}\bigg)=\frac{\left|\log b\right|^{\eta_{i}}}{\lambda}\bigg(b_{s}-\frac{b\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+\frac{\eta_{i}b_{s}}{\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\bigg(1-\frac{\eta_{i}}{\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)\frac{\left|\log b\right|^{\eta_{i}}}{\lambda}\bigg[b_{s}+\bigg(1+\frac{\eta_{i}}{\left|\log b\right|}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)b^{2}\bigg]\,\begin{cases}\leq 0,\;\;i=1,\\
\geq 0,\;\;i=2,\end{cases}

where we see the choice of
\eta_{i}
is decisive to the sign. By direct integrations, it implies

\frac{b(0)}{\lambda(0)}\left|\frac{\log b(0)}{\log b(t)}\right|^{\eta_{2}}\leq\frac{b(t)}{\lambda(t)}\leq\frac{b(0)}{\lambda(0)}\left|\frac{\log b(0)}{\log b(t)}\right|^{\eta_{1}},

(5.97)

from which we have

\frac{\lambda(t)^{6}}{\lambda(0)^{6}}\mathcal{E}_{4}(0)\leq\frac{b(t)^{6}\left|\log b(t)\right|^{6\eta_{2}}}{b(0)^{6}\left|\log b(0)\right|^{6\eta_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{4}(0)\leq b(t)^{5}\ll\frac{b(t)^{4}}{\left|\log b(t)\right|^{2}}.

(5.98)

For the second part on the RHS of (
5.96
), it follows from (
4.34
) that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &b=-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+O(b^{3})\leq-\big(1+2b(0)^{2}\big)\lambda\lambda_{t},\\
&-b_{t}=-\frac{b_{s}}{\lambda^{2}}\leq\bigg(1+\frac{2}{\left|\log b(0)\right|}\bigg)\frac{b^{2}}{\lambda^{2}},\\
&4+\frac{1}{\left|\log b\right|}\leq 4+\frac{1}{\left|\log b(0)\right|},\end{aligned}\right.

using which we compute

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{b^{5}}{\lambda^{8}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle d\sigma\leq-\big(1+2b(0)^{2}\big)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\lambda_{t}}{\lambda^{7}}\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}d\sigma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\big(1+2b(0)^{2}\big)\bigg[\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{6}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg|_{0}^{t}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{b_{t}b^{3}}{\lambda^{6}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg(4+\frac{1}{\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)d\sigma\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\big(1+2b(0)^{2}\big)\bigg[\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{6}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg|_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle+\left(1+\frac{2}{\left|\log b(0)\right|}\right)\left(4+\frac{1}{\left|\log b(0)\right|}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{b^{5}}{\lambda^{8}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}d\sigma\bigg],

and hence

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{b^{5}}{\lambda^{8}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{2}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{\left|\log b(0)\right|}\bigg)\bigg)\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{6}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg|_{0}^{t}

(5.99)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{2}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{\left|\log b(0)\right|}\bigg)\bigg)\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{6}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}.

Injecting (
5.98
), (
5.99
) into (
5.96
), we obtain

\mathcal{E}_{4}(t)\leq\big((1-d_{2})K+C\big)\frac{b(t)^{4}}{\left|\log b(t)\right|^{2}},

for some constant
C>0
independent of
K,M
. By choosing
K>0
large enough, we see there exist
0<\eta<1
such that
(1-d_{2})K+C\leq K(1-\eta)
, and thus the desired bound (
4.21
) follows.

Step 5
.
The refined bound of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
. A direct interpolation between the bounds of
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{4}
is insufficient to show (
4.20
). Thus we treat it attentively. By (
2.11
), (
2.16
), we see

u\wedge\Delta u=u\wedge\big(\Delta u+|\nabla Q|^{2}u\big)={\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w},

(5.100)

from which we compute:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int\big|u\wedge\Delta u\big|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\rho_{2}\int(u\wedge\Delta u)\cdot\big[u\wedge(u\wedge\Delta u)\big]\wedge\Delta u

(5.101)

\displaystyle+\rho_{1}\int(u\wedge\Delta u)\cdot u\wedge\Delta(u\wedge\Delta u)

\displaystyle-\rho_{2}\int(u\wedge\Delta u)\cdot u\wedge\Delta\big[u\wedge(u\wedge\Delta u)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{4}}\;\bigg\{\rho_{2}\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\cdot({\rm\hat{J}}^{2}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w})\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}

\displaystyle-\rho_{1}\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\cdot({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\hat{w}+\rho_{2}\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}({\rm\hat{J}}^{2}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w})\bigg\}.

Note that the LHS is a rough equivalent of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
disregarding the
\lambda
power

\lambda^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\int\hskip-1.00006pt\left|u\wedge\Delta u\right|^{2}=\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\int\hskip-1.00006pt\big|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\big|^{2}=\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\int\hskip-1.00006pt\big|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w\big|^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptO\bigg(\hskip-1.00006pt\int\big|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\big|^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\bigg)=\mathcal{E}_{2}+O\big(b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\big).

(5.102)

In addition, from (
4.15
) and Appendix
A
, we can estimate each term on the RHS of (
5.101
). Indeed, by (
5.53
), (
A.11
), (
A.15
), (
A.21
), the first term is bounded by

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\cdot({\rm\hat{J}}^{2}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w})\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}

\displaystyle=\int{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\cdot\big({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}^{2}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\big)=\int\big({\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\cdot(Q+\hat{w})\big)\big|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big(\|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim b\Big(b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2}+Kb^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{6}\Big)\lesssim Kb^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{6}.

Using Lemma
5.4
, (
A.31
), (
A.34
), we estimate the second one

\displaystyle-\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\cdot({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\hat{w}

\displaystyle=-\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\cdot({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\tilde{w}_{0}-\int w_{2}\cdot({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\tilde{w}_{0}

\displaystyle-\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}-\int w_{2}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}

\displaystyle\leq b\,\|{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm\hat{J}}w_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\|{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|w_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{2}+2\sqrt{K}b^{3}+Kb^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{2}\lesssim Kb^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{2}.

Similarly, from (
A.27
), (
A.34
), the third term is bounded by

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}({\rm\hat{J}}^{2}{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w})

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\|{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\it\mathbb{H}}w\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\Big(\|{\it\mathbb{H}}({\rm\hat{J}}^{2}{\it\mathbb{H}}\tilde{w}_{0})\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\it\mathbb{H}}({\rm\hat{J}}w_{2})\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(b\left|\log b\right|+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\Big)\frac{\sqrt{K}b^{2}}{\left|\log b\right|}\lesssim Kb^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{2}.

We insert these bounds into (
5.101
) to obtain

\frac{d}{dt}\int\big|u\wedge\Delta u\big|^{2}\lesssim\frac{Kb^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}.

Integrating this in time from
0
to
t
and applying (
5.102
), we have

\mathcal{E}_{2}(t)\lesssim b(t)^{2}\left|\log b(t)\right|^{2}+\lambda(t)^{2}b(0)^{10}+K\lambda(t)^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{b(\sigma)^{3}\left|\log b(\sigma)\right|^{3}}{\lambda(\sigma)^{4}}{\rm d}\sigma.

(5.103)

From (
5.97
), there holds

\lambda(t)^{2}b(0)^{10}\leq b(0)^{10}\bigg(b(t)\frac{\lambda(0)}{b(0)}\bigg|\frac{\log b(t)}{\log b(0)}\bigg|^{\eta_{2}}\bigg)^{2}\lesssim b(t)^{2}\left|\log b(t)\right|^{5}.

Moreover, from (
4.34
), we have
b^{2}\lesssim-b_{s}
, which together with (
5.97
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{b^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{3}}{\lambda^{4}}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{bb_{s}\left|\log b\right|^{3}}{\lambda^{4}}d\sigma\lesssim-\frac{b(0)^{2}\left|\log b(0)\right|^{2\eta_{1}}}{\lambda(0)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{b_{t}}{b\left|\log b\right|^{2\eta_{1}-3}}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{b(0)^{2}\left|\log b(0)\right|^{2}}{\lambda(0)^{2}}\lesssim\frac{b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2\eta_{2}}}{\lambda^{2}\left|\log b(0)\right|^{2\eta_{2}-2}}\lesssim\frac{b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{5}}{\lambda^{2}}.

Injecting these into (
5.103
), we obtain

\mathcal{E}_{2}\lesssim Kb^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{5}\leq\frac{K}{2}b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{6},

where, in view of (
5.89
), the last inequality holds if
b(0)
is chosen small enough. This is the refined bound (
4.20
), and thus ends the proof.
\square

Remark 5.3

(i)
In the above proof we have actually specified the constants
M,K
. To be explicit, we first let
M\gg 1
be a large constant compared with those implicit constants from earlier estimates. Next the constant
b^{\ast}=b^{\ast}(M)
is chosen to be a small upper bound of
b(t)
, making
b(t)
an higher-order infinitesimal relative to
\frac{1}{\log M}
. Then we set
K\gg 1
in comparison with other unnamed constants, such that the bootstrap bounds (
4.14
)–(
4.16
) and (
4.13
) for
b(t)
hold.
(ii)
We note that the initial bounds (
4.9
), (
4.11
) imply an open initial data set of
b,w
stable under small perturbation, while
a(0)
requires precise selection relying on
b(0),w(0)
, through a topological argument
(
see (
5.91
), (
5.92
)
)
. This actually yields a codimension one initial data set of
u
, as mentioned in Theorem
1.1
.

6
Description on the singularity formation

We are now ready to prove the Theorem
1.1
. In this subsection, we prove the finite time blowup, show the convergence of the phase
\varTheta
, give a sharp description on the blowup speed
\lambda
, and prove the strong convergence of the excess energy.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

By Proposition
4.1
,
w
has been trapped in the regime of (
4.13
), (
4.14
)–(
4.16
). Thus
u
satisfying (
4.2
) exists for
s\in[s_{0},+\infty)
. Now we suppose the lifespan of
u
is
T\leq+\infty
, and study the corresponding asymptotic behavior.

Step 1
.
Finite time blowup
. We apply (
5.97
) and
\lambda_{s}/\lambda\lesssim-b
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\sqrt{\lambda}=\frac{\lambda_{s}}{2\lambda^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\lesssim-\frac{b}{\lambda\sqrt{\lambda}}\lesssim-\frac{1}{\sqrt{b}}\bigg(\frac{b(0)}{\lambda(0)}\bigg|\frac{\log b(0)}{\log b}\bigg|^{\eta_{2}}\bigg)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

By the smallness and monotonicity of
b
(
5.89
), there exists constant
C(u_{0})>0
such that

\frac{d}{dt}\sqrt{\lambda}<-C(u_{0})<0,

which together with (
5.97
) implies

T<+\infty,\quad\mbox{with}\quad\lambda(T)=b(T)=0.

(6.1)

Thus the scaling
\lambda(t)\to 0
as
t\to T
, and the solution
u
blows up at
T
.

Step 2
.
Convergence of the phase
. As illustrated in Remark
4.2
, the modulation equations (
4.34
) on parameter
a
is too crude to show the convergence of the phase. We claim the following refined bound with additional logarithmic gain

|a(t)|\lesssim C(\delta)\frac{b(t)}{\left|\log b(t)\right|^{\frac{3}{2}}},

(6.2)

for some universal small constant
\delta>0
and a large constant
C(\delta)
. Applying (
6.2
) with (
5.89
), we obtain

\lim_{s\to+\infty}\varTheta(s)-\varTheta(s_{0})\leq\int_{s_{0}}^{+\infty}|a(s)|\,ds\leq\int_{s_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{ds}{s(\log s)^{\frac{3}{2}}}<+\infty,

which implies the convergence of the phase

\varTheta(t)\to\varTheta(T)\in\mathbb{R},\;\;\mbox{as}\;\;t\to T.

It remains to prove (
6.2
). To this end, we choose the varying scale

B_{\delta}=b^{-\delta},

for sufficiently small constant
\delta>0
, and repeat the computations as in Proposition
4.2
. More precisely, we define the direction

\varPhi_{\delta}=\chi_{B_{\delta}}\varLambda\phi\begin{bmatrix}\rho_{1}\\
\rho_{2}\\
0\end{bmatrix},\quad\mbox{with}\quad\left\{\begin{aligned} &\,\|\varPhi_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}}\sim\sqrt{\left|\log b\right|},\\
&\,(\varPhi_{1,0},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta})\sim\left|\log b\right|,\\
&\,(\varPhi_{0,1},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta})=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(6.3)

We take the scalar products of (
4.22
) with
\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}
, and the resulting equation is

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\big(\partial_{s}w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\big(\varLambda w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)+\big(\rho_{1}{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w-\rho_{2}{\rm\hat{J}}^{2}{\it\mathbb{H}}w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(\widetilde{{\rm Mod}},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)+\big(\tilde{\varPsi}_{0},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)+\big(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big).

Using Appendix
A
, we have

\Big|\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Big|\,\big|\big(\varLambda w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)\big|\lesssim b^{1-C\delta}\bigg(\int\frac{|\mathbb{H^{\perp}}(\varLambda w)|^{2}}{(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim b^{1-C\delta}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{4}}.

Recalling (
4.40
), (
4.41
), we see the linear terms are bounded by

\big|\big({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)\big|+\big|\big({\rm\hat{J}}^{2}{\it\mathbb{H}}w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\left|\log b\right|}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{4}},

where the
\sqrt{\left|\log b\right|}
comes from the
L^{2}
norm of
\varPhi_{\delta}
. Moreover, from (
3.54
), (
4.34
), and also the orthogonality of
\varPhi_{0,1}
and
\varPhi_{\delta}
(
6.3
), we observe

\big(\widetilde{{\rm Mod}},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)=\big({\rm Mod},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)=a_{s}\big(\varPhi_{1,0},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)+O\big(b^{1-C\delta}U(t)\big).

The estimate for
\tilde{\varPsi}_{0}
follows from (
3.57
), and the bound for
\mathcal{R}
can be obtained by simply repeating the computation (
4.42
), (
4.43
), with an extra
b^{1+C\delta}
smallness from the
L^{2}
norm of
\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}
. Combining these bounds, we obtain

a_{s}\big(\varPhi_{1,0},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)+\big(\partial_{s}w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta}\big)\lesssim\frac{b^{2}}{\left|\log b\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(6.4)

Now we let

\tilde{a}=a+\frac{(w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta})}{(\varPhi_{1,0},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta})}=a+O\bigg(b^{-C\delta}\frac{b^{2}}{\left|\log b\right|}\bigg).

and its derivative follows from (
6.4
):

\tilde{a}_{s}=a_{s}+\frac{(\partial_{s}w,\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta})}{(\varPhi_{1,0},\mathbb{H^{\perp}}\varPhi_{\delta})}+O\bigg(\frac{b^{3-C\delta}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg)\lesssim\frac{b^{2}}{\left|\log b\right|^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Integrating this from
s=+\infty
where
\tilde{a}=0
to the present time
s
using (
5.89
), we obtain the bound

|\tilde{a}|\lesssim\frac{b}{\left|\log b\right|^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

This yields the corresponding bound for
a
(
6.2
).

Step 3
.
Derivation of the blowup speed
. We recall the modulation equations (
4.34
) that

b_{s}=-b^{2}\bigg(1+\frac{2}{\left|\log b\right|}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{b^{2}}{\sqrt{\log M}\left|\log b\right|}\bigg),

From the rough asymptotics (
5.89
), we assume
b=1/s+f/s^{2}
with
|f|\ll s
, and
s
sufficiently large to derive the refined estimate

b(s)=\frac{1}{s}-\frac{2}{s\log s}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{s(\log s)^{2}}\bigg).

Moreover, from
b+\lambda_{s}/\lambda=O(b^{3})
, we see the concentration scale
\lambda
admits

\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}=-\frac{1}{s}+\frac{2}{s\log s}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{s(\log s)^{2}}\bigg),

(6.5)

from which we have

\frac{1}{s}=C(u_{0})\big(1+o(1)\big)\frac{\lambda}{\left|\log\lambda\right|^{2}}.

Using
-1/s\sim\lambda\lambda_{t}
by (
6.5
), we get

\lambda_{t}=-C(u_{0})\big(1+o(1)\big)\frac{1}{\left|\log\lambda\right|^{2}}.

(6.6)

Integrating this from
t
to
T
, the blowup speed (
1.8
) follows

\lambda(t)=C(u_{0})\big(1+o(1)\big)\frac{(T-t)}{\left|\log(T-t)\right|^{2}}.

(6.7)

Combining (
6.6
) with (
6.7
), and applying
b+\lambda_{s}/\lambda=O(b^{3})
again, we obtain

b(t)=C(u_{0})\big(1+o(1)\big)\frac{(T-t)}{\left|\log(T-t)\right|^{4}}.

(6.8)

Step 4
.
Convergence of the excess energy
. From (
4.2
) we have the decomposition

u=e^{\varTheta{\rm R}}Q_{\lambda}+\tilde{u},\quad\mbox{where}\quad\tilde{u}:=e^{\varTheta{\rm R}}\hat{v}_{\lambda}.

Owing to the dissipative Dirichlet energy (
1.3
) and the energy-critical scaling, the
\dot{H}^{1}
norm of
\tilde{u}
is bounded

\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla(e^{\varTheta{\rm R}}Q_{\lambda})\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla Q\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim C(u_{0}).

Moreover, we have the
\dot{H}^{2}
bound

\|\Delta\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim C(u_{0}).

(6.9)

Indeed, from (
2.16
), (
2.26
), (
A.27
), we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}}=\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\int|\Delta\hat{v}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}\hat{w}|^{2}+\int\frac{|\hat{w}|^{2}}{1+y^{8}}\bigg)\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\big(\mathcal{E}_{2}+b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2}\big).

(6.10)

The inequality (
5.103
) with the explicit asymptotics (
6.7
), (
6.8
) gives

\mathcal{E}_{2}\lesssim b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2}+\lambda^{2}b(0)^{10}+\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{dt}{(T-t)\left|\log(T-t)\right|^{2}}\lesssim b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2}+\lambda^{2}.

Injecting this into (
6.10
) with an application of (
5.97
) yields the desired
\dot{H}^{2}
boundedness. By a simple localization process, this boundedness yields a strong convergence of
\nabla\tilde{u}
outside the blowup point (the origin). More precisely, there exists
u^{\ast}\in\dot{H}^{1}
, such that for any
R>0
there holds

\|\nabla u-\nabla u^{\ast}\|_{L^{2}(|x|>R)}\sim\|\nabla\tilde{u}-\nabla u^{\ast}\|_{L^{2}(|x|>R)}\to 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad t\to T,

which yields (
1.7
). Now the convergence (
1.7
) together with the
\dot{H}^{2}
bound (
6.9
) gives (
1.10
). This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

\square

Appendix A
Coercivity estimates and interpolation estimates

In this appendix, we list some results on the coercivity of the Hamiltonians
H,H^{2}
, and also the interpolation estimates used in the proof of Proposition
4.2
,
5.1
. The complete proof can be found in
[
34
]
.

Lemma A.1
(Coercivity of
H

[
34
]
)

Let
M\geq 1
be a large enough universal constant. Let
\varPhi_{M}
be given by (
4.5
). Then there exists a universal constant
C(M)>0
such that for all radially symmetric function
u\in H^{1}
satisfying

\int\frac{|u|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}+\int\big|\partial_{y}(Au)\big|^{2}<+\infty.

and the orthogonality conditions

(u,\varPhi_{M})=0,

there holds

\displaystyle\int_{y\geq 1}\frac{|\partial_{y}^{2}u|^{2}}{1+\left|\log y\right|^{2}}+\int\frac{|\partial_{y}u|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}+\int\frac{|u|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}

(A.1)

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\bigg[\int\frac{|Au|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}+\int\big|\partial_{y}(Au)\big|^{2}\bigg]\lesssim C(M)\int|Hu|^{2}.

Lemma A.2
(Coercivity of
H^{2}

[
34
]
)

Assume the conditions in Lemma
A.1
. Then there exists a universal constant
C(M)>0
such that for all radially symmetric function
u
satisfying

\displaystyle\int|\partial_{y}AHu|^{2}+\int\frac{|AHu|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+y^{2})}+\int\frac{|Hu|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\int\frac{|u|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}+\int\frac{(\partial_{y}u)^{2}}{y^{2}(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}<+\infty.

and the orthogonality conditions

(u,\varPhi_{M})=0,\quad(Hu,\varPhi_{M})=0,

there holds

\displaystyle\int\frac{|Hu|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}+\int\frac{|\partial_{y}Hu|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}+\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{4}u|^{2}}{(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}

(A.2)

\displaystyle+\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{3}u|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}+\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{2}u|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\int\frac{|\partial_{y}u|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}+\int\frac{|u|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|)^{2}}\leq C(M)\int|H^{2}u|^{2}.

We recall the notations

w=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha\\
\beta\\
\gamma\end{bmatrix}=w^{\perp}+\gamma\,e_{z},\qquad w_{2}={\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w^{\perp}=w_{2}^{0}+w_{2}^{1}.

and assume the bootstrap bounds (
4.14
)–(
4.16
), then the following interpolation bounds are the consequences of the coercivity estimate (
A.1
), (
A.2
) and the regularity of
w
at the origin ensured by the smoothness of the LL flow
u
.

Lemma A.3
(Interpolation bounds for
w^{\perp}

[
34
]
)

There holds:

\displaystyle\int\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{|w^{\perp}|^{2}}{y^{4}(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pty^{4})(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\left|\log y\right|^{2})}+\hskip-1.00006pt\int\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}w^{\perp}|^{2}}{y^{2}(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pty^{6-2i})(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\lesssim C(M)\mathcal{E}_{4},\quad 1\leq i\leq 3,

(A.3)

\displaystyle\int_{|y|\geq 1}\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}w^{\perp}|^{2}}{(1+y^{4-2i})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\lesssim C(M)\mathcal{E}_{2},\quad 1\leq i\leq 2,

(A.4)

\displaystyle\int_{|y|\geq 1}\frac{(1+\left|\log y\right|^{C})|\partial_{y}^{i}w^{\perp}|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})(1+y^{6-2i})}\lesssim b^{4}\left|\log b\right|^{C_{1}(C)},\quad 0\leq i\leq 3,

(A.5)

\displaystyle\int_{y\geq 1}\frac{1+\left|\log y\right|^{C}}{y^{2}(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})(1+y^{4-2i})}|\partial_{y}^{i}w^{\perp}|^{2}\lesssim b^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{C_{1}(C)},\quad 0\leq i\leq 2,

(A.6)

\displaystyle\int_{|y|\geq 1}|\partial_{y}{\it\mathbb{H}}w^{\perp}|^{2}\lesssim b^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{6},

(A.7)

\displaystyle\big\|w^{\perp}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast}),

(A.8)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{A}w^{\perp}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lesssim b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{9},

(A.9)

\displaystyle\int_{y\leq 1}\frac{|\mathbb{A}w^{\perp}|^{2}}{y^{6}(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\lesssim C(M)\mathcal{E}_{4},

(A.10)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mathbb{A}w^{\perp}}{y^{2}(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\left|\log y\right|)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\left\|\frac{\Delta\mathbb{A}w^{\perp}}{1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\left|\log y\right|}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt+\left\|\frac{{\it\mathbb{H}}w^{\perp}}{y(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\left|\log y\right|)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\lesssim b^{4},

(A.11)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{|H\alpha|+|H\beta|}{y(1+\left|\log y\right|)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}\lesssim b^{4},

(A.12)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{w^{\perp}}{y}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{y}w^{\perp}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\leq 1)}^{2}\lesssim b^{4},

(A.13)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{w^{\perp}}{y}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\geq 1)}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{y}w^{\perp}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\geq 1)}^{2}\lesssim b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{8},

(A.14)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{w^{\perp}}{1+y^{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\perp}}{1+y}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\perp}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(y\geq 1)}^{2}\lesssim C(M)\,b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2}.

(A.15)

Lemma A.4
(Interpolation bounds for
\gamma

[
34
]
)

There holds:

\displaystyle\int\frac{|\gamma|^{2}}{y^{6}(1+y^{2})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}+\int\frac{|\partial_{y}\gamma|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+y^{4-2i})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{i}\gamma|^{2}}{y^{2}(1+y^{6-2i})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg),\quad 2\leq i\leq 3,

(A.16)

\displaystyle\int_{y\geq 1}\frac{1+\left|\log y\right|^{C}}{y^{4}(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})(1+y^{4-2i})}|\partial_{y}^{i}\gamma|^{2}\lesssim b^{4}\left|\log b\right|^{C_{1}(C)},\quad 0\leq i\leq 2,

(A.17)

\displaystyle\int_{y\geq 1}\frac{1+\left|\log y\right|^{C}}{y^{6-2i}(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}|\partial_{y}^{i}\gamma|^{2}\lesssim b^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{C_{1}(C)},\quad 0\leq i\leq 2,

(A.18)

\displaystyle\int\frac{|A\partial_{y}\gamma|^{2}}{y^{4}(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg),

(A.19)

\displaystyle\left\|\gamma\frac{1+|y|}{|y|}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast}),

(A.20)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{(1+|y|)\gamma}{y^{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{y}\gamma\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lesssim b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{8},

(A.21)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\gamma}{y(1+y)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}\gamma}{y}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lesssim C(M)b^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{2}.

(A.22)

\displaystyle\int|\Delta\gamma|^{2}\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast})\mathcal{E}_{2}+b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2},

(A.23)

\displaystyle\|\Delta\gamma\|_{L^{\infty}(y\geq 1)}^{2}\lesssim b^{3}\left|\log b\right|^{8},

(A.24)

\displaystyle\int|w^{\perp}|^{2}|\Delta^{2}\gamma|^{2}+\int_{y\geq 1}|\Delta^{2}\gamma|^{2}\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast})\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\bigg).

(A.25)

Lemma A.5
(Interpolation bounds for
w_{2}

[
34
]
)

There holds:

\displaystyle\int|w_{2}|^{2}=\mathcal{E}_{2}+O\big(b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2}+\delta(b^{\ast})\mathcal{E}_{2}\big),

(A.26)

\displaystyle\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}w|^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{2}+b^{2}\left|\log b\right|^{2},

(A.27)

\displaystyle\int\frac{|{\it\mathbb{H}}w|^{2}}{(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\lesssim C(M)\mathcal{E}_{4},

(A.28)

\displaystyle\int\frac{|w_{2}^{0}|^{2}}{(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\lesssim C(M)\mathcal{E}_{4},

(A.29)

\displaystyle\int\frac{|w_{2}^{1}|^{2}}{(1+y^{4})(1+\left|\log y\right|^{2})}\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast})\left(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\right),

(A.30)

\displaystyle\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}|^{2}\lesssim C(M)\left(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\right),

(A.31)

\displaystyle\int\big|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}\big|^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}},

(A.32)

\displaystyle\int\big|{\it\mathbb{H}}w_{2}^{1}\big|^{2}+\int\left|{\rm R}{\it\mathbb{H}}\big({\rm R}^{2}w_{2}^{1}\big)\right|^{2}\lesssim\delta(b^{\ast})\left(\mathcal{E}_{4}+\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}\right),

(A.33)

\displaystyle\int|{\it\mathbb{H}}{\rm\hat{J}}w_{2}|^{2}\lesssim C(M)\frac{b^{4}}{\left|\log b\right|^{2}}.

(A.34)

Appendix B
Proof of Lemma
5.4

This appendix is devoted to prove Lemma
5.4
. The proof is basically algebraic. The key here is to explore the structure of the LHS of (
5.61
).

Proof of Lemma
5.4
.

Step 1
.
Gain of two derivatives
. Let

a=\alpha e_{r}+\beta e_{\tau}+\gamma\hskip 0.50003ptQ,\quad\varGamma=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha\\
\beta\\
\gamma\end{bmatrix},

be a decomposition of the vector
a
under the Frenet basis, with its components
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
functions of the radial variable
y
satisfying the constraint

\alpha^{2}+\beta^{2}+(1+\gamma)^{2}=1.

To ease the notations, we introduce the vectors

Z_{1}=(1+Z)e_{y},\quad Z_{2}=\frac{Z}{y}e_{z}-(1+Z)e_{x},

based on which we apply Lemma
2.1
and obtain

\partial_{y}a=\partial_{y}\varGamma+Z_{1}\wedge\varGamma,\quad\partial_{\tau}a=Z_{2}\wedge\varGamma.

(B.1)

We also recall the double wedge formula

a\wedge(b\wedge c)=(a\cdot c)b-(a\cdot b)c.

In view of (
2.16
), (
2.19
), we compute

-\varGamma\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma=-\varGamma\cdot(e_{z}+\hat{w})\wedge{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma=a\cdot\hat{v}\wedge(\Delta a+|\nabla Q|^{2}a)=a\cdot\big(\hat{v}\wedge\Delta a\big).

(B.2)

which together with the action of the Laplacian in the Frenet basis gives

\displaystyle\int\hskip-1.00006pta\cdot\big(\hat{v}\wedge\Delta a\big)=\int\hskip-1.00006pt\Delta a\cdot\big(a\wedge\hat{v}\big)=\int\hskip-1.00006pta\cdot\Delta\big(a\wedge\hat{v}\big)=\int\hskip-1.00006pta\cdot\big(\Delta a\wedge\hat{v}+2\nabla a\wedge\nabla\hat{v}\big),

and thus

\int\hskip-1.00006pta\cdot\big(\hat{v}\wedge\Delta a\big)=\int\hskip-1.00006pta\cdot\big(\nabla a\wedge\nabla\hat{v}\big).

(B.3)

Combining (
B.1
), (
B.2
),
(
B.3
), we let
\varGamma={\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma
, and compute the LHS of (
5.61
):

\displaystyle-\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\varGamma=

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\partial_{y}{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\wedge\partial_{y}\hat{w}+\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\big(Z_{1}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\wedge\partial_{y}\hat{w}

(B.4)

\displaystyle+\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\partial_{y}{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\wedge\big(Z_{1}\wedge(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big)

\displaystyle+\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\big(Z_{1}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\wedge\big(Z_{1}\wedge(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big)

\displaystyle+\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\big(Z_{2}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\wedge\big(Z_{2}\wedge(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big),

which gives a two-derivatives gain. The normalization of
(e_{z}+\hat{w})
and the structures of
Z_{1},Z_{2}
produce some cancellations in the RHS of (
B.4
). To see this, we Let

\varGamma_{2}={\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma,

(B.5)

then the first term on the RHS of (
B.4
) can be simplified to

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\wedge\partial_{y}\hat{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\big[\partial_{y}\hat{w}\wedge\varGamma_{2}+(e_{z}+\hat{w})\wedge\partial_{y}\varGamma_{2}\big]\wedge\partial_{y}\hat{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\varGamma_{2}\big)\big(\partial_{y}\hat{w}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)-\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)\big(\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)

\displaystyle+\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}\varGamma_{2}\big)\big((e_{z}+\hat{w})\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)-\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big)\big(\partial_{y}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)\big(\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big).

The second term on the RHS of (
B.4
) is

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\big(Z_{1}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\wedge\partial_{y}\hat{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\Big[{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big(Z_{1}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)-Z_{1}\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int(1+Z)\big|{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big|^{2}(e_{y}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w})-\int(1+Z)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\big(\partial_{y}\hat{w}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big).

Moreover, the third term is

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\partial_{y}{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\wedge\big(Z_{1}\wedge(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot Z_{1})\big(\partial_{y}{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big)-\big({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big)\big(\partial_{y}{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot e_{y}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int(1+Z)\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot e_{y}\big)\Big[\big(\partial_{y}\hat{w}\wedge\varGamma_{2}+{\rm\hat{J}}\partial_{y}\varGamma_{2}\big)\cdot(e_{z}+\hat{w})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int(1+Z)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\big(\partial_{y}\hat{w}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big).

We observe

Z_{1}\wedge e_{z}=(1+Z)e_{x},\quad Z_{2}\wedge e_{z}=(1+Z)e_{y},

(B.6)

then the last two terms of (
B.4
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\big(Z_{1}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\wedge\big(Z_{1}\wedge(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big)+\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\big(Z_{2}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\wedge\big(Z_{2}\wedge(e_{z}+\hat{w})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot Z_{1}\big)\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot Z_{1}\wedge e_{z}\big)+\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\big(Z_{1}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\wedge\big(Z_{1}\wedge\hat{w}\big)

\displaystyle-\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot Z_{2}\big)\big({\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot Z_{2}\wedge e_{z}\big)+\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\big(Z_{2}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\wedge\big(Z_{2}\wedge\hat{w}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\frac{Z(1+Z)}{y}\big(e_{z}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\big(Z_{1}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\wedge\big(Z_{1}\wedge\hat{w}\big)+\int{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\cdot\big(Z_{2}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}\varGamma_{2}\big)\wedge\big(Z_{2}\wedge\hat{w}\big)

Injecting these computations into (
B.4
), we have the following refined formula

\displaystyle-\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}})^{2}\varGamma

(B.7)

\displaystyle=-\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)\big({\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)+\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\big(e_{y}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma|^{2}

\displaystyle-2\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\big(\partial_{y}\hat{w}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)-\int\frac{Z(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)}{y}\big(e_{z}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)

\displaystyle+\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\big(Z_{1}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\wedge\big(Z_{1}\wedge\hat{w}\big)+\int{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\big(Z_{2}\wedge{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\wedge\big(Z_{2}\wedge\hat{w}\big).

Step 2
.
Extraction of the leading terms
. To derive precise bounds for the RHS of (
B.7
), let us introduce the following decomposition of
\hat{w},\tilde{w}_{0}
(This is different from decomposition (
3.18
)):

\hat{w}=\tilde{w}_{0}^{1}+\tilde{w}_{0}^{2}+w,\quad\mbox{with}\quad\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{w}_{0}^{1}=b\hskip 0.50003pt\tilde{\varPhi}_{0,1}=b\hskip 0.50003pt\chi_{B_{1}}\varPhi_{0,1},\\
&\tilde{w}_{0}^{2}=\tilde{w}_{0}-\tilde{w}_{0}^{1}.\end{aligned}\right.

(B.8)

By (
A.13
), (
A.21
), the first term on the RHS of (
B.7
) is bounded by

\Big|\hskip-1.00006pt\int\big({\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)\big({\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)\Big|\leq\,\|\partial_{y}\hat{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\,\|{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

According to (
B.8
), we apply (
A.15
), (
A.22
), and
|a|\leq b\left|\log b\right|^{-1}
to compute the second term

\displaystyle\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\big(e_{y}\cdot\partial_{y}\hat{w}\big)|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle b\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\,\partial_{y}\tilde{\varPhi}_{0,1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(2)$}}}}}}\,|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma|^{2}+\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\Big(e_{y}\cdot\partial_{y}(\tilde{w}_{0}^{2}+w)\Big)|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle b\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\,\partial_{y}\tilde{\varPhi}_{0,1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(2)$}}}}}}\,|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma|^{2}+O\Big(b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\,\|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big).

Similarly, the third term on the RHS of (
B.7
) is

\displaystyle-2\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\big(\partial_{y}\hat{w}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2b\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\big(\partial_{y}\tilde{\varPhi}_{0,1}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)

\displaystyle-2\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\Big(\partial_{y}(\tilde{w}_{0}^{2}+w)\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2b\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\,\partial_{y}\tilde{\varPhi}_{0,1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\,\big(e_{x}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)

\displaystyle-2b\int(1\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006ptZ)\,\partial_{y}\tilde{\varPhi}_{0,1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{868894}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26665pt}{\resizebox{759247}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.22499pt}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-2.26666pt}{\resizebox{759248}{445645}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle(2)$}}}}}}\,\big(e_{y}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\big)^{2}+O\Big(b\,\delta(b^{\ast})\,\|{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big).

Moreover, from (
2.25
) we note the simple identities

\displaystyle e_{z}\cdot{\rm\hat{J}}{\it\mathbb{H}}\varGamma
